<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct a finitely presented torsion-free counterexample to Kaplansky's direct finiteness conjecture over the field of two elements. The group admits a finite two-dimensional classifying complex.

- **thm-main** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There exist a finitely presented torsion-free group $G$ and elements $a,b,c\in\mathbb F_2[G]$ such that $$ab=1,\qquad ac=0,\qquad c\ne0.$$ Consequently $ba\ne1$. The group $G$ admits a finite two-dimensional classifying complex.
  - Explanation: A right inverse exists for the constructed group-algebra element, while the nonzero annihilated element excludes a left inverse.
- **prop-parity** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-parity)): Let $\Gamma_A,\Gamma_B$ be finite loopless graphs immersed in the same rose, with roots and cone group as above. Suppose that $$\label{eq:parity-assumptions} \begin{aligned} \|S_x\cap S_{x'}\|&\equiv1\pmod2 &&\bigl(x,x'\in A',\ (x,x')\ne(x_A,x_A)\bigr),\\ \|S_{x_A}\|&\equiv0\pmod2,\\ \|S_x\cap S_y\|&\equiv1\pmod2 &&\bigl(x\in A',\ y\in B'\bigr). \end{aligned}$$ Suppose also that the roots are protected in the following sense: $$\label{eq:root-protection} \begin{gathered} \text{if a path in $\Gamma$ starts at $x_A$ or $x_B$ and ends}\\[-2pt] \text{at a different vertex, its label is nontrivial in $G$.} \end{gathered}$$ Then the finite scalar sums $$\label{eq:scalar-elements} a=\sum_{x\in A'}g_x,\qquad b=\sum_{x\in A'}g_x^{-1},\qquad c=\sum_{y\in B'}h_y^{-1} \quad\text{in }\mathbb F_2[G]$$ satisfy $ab=1$, $ac=0$, and $c\ne0$. Consequently $ba\ne1$.
  - Explanation: Odd-degree component parity cancels the mixed product and leaves the identity in the self product; root protection preserves a nonzero coefficient.
- **prop-types** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-types)): For all sufficiently large $m\equiv1\pmod4$, the prescribed sets $S_x\subseteq T$, $x\in A\sqcup B$, satisfy the following identities. For every $Y\in\{A,B\}$ and $t\in T$, $$\label{eq:label-balance} \bigl\|\{x\in Y:t\in S_x\}\bigr\|=\|Y\|p.$$ Their intersection cardinalities satisfy $$\label{eq:type-parity} \begin{aligned} \|S_x\cap S_{x'}\|&\equiv1\pmod2 &&\bigl(x,x'\in A,\ (x,x')\ne(x_A,x_A)\bigr),\\ \|S_{x_A}\|&\equiv0\pmod2,\\ \|S_x\cap S_y\|&\equiv1\pmod2 &&\bigl(x\in A,\ y\in B\bigr). \end{aligned}$$ Moreover, $129\le \|S_x\|\le136$ at every vertex.
  - Explanation: Projective-plane incidences and complements of Fano lines give balanced label counts and the distinguished parity exception.
- **lem-word-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-word-decay)): There is a constant $\delta>0$, depending only on the prescribed alphabet and weights, such that for all sufficiently large integers $h$, $$\label{eq:word-decay} \sum_{W\in T^h}P(W)^2\le \exp(-2\delta h).$$
  - Explanation: A positive vector bounds the squared turn-weight matrix by a strict contraction, giving exponential decay.
- **lem-prescriptions** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-prescriptions)): There is a nonnegative sequence $r_n=vp+O(d_*^{2L+2})=o(n)$ with the following property. If $\mathcal S$ is a collection of $s$ distinct prescriptions having positive probability under $\mathbb P_L$, and $e$ is a further prescription not in $\mathcal S$, then $$\label{eq:one-prescription} \mathbb P_L(e\mid\mathcal S) \le\frac1{np-s-r_n} \qquad\text{whenever }np-s-r_n>0.$$ In particular, for every fixed $C_1>0$, a specified collection of $E\le C_1L$ distinct prescriptions satisfies the uniform bound $$\label{eq:short-prescriptions} \mathbb P_L(\text{all }E\text{ prescriptions hold}) \le (np)^{-E}\exp(o(L)).$$
  - Explanation: Injective switching bounds additional edge prescriptions under girth conditioning, without a lower bound on the conditioning probability.
- **prop-diameter** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-diameter)): There is a fixed constant $d>0$ such that, with $\mathbb P_L$-probability tending to one as $m\to\infty$, every connected component of $\Gamma_A$ and $\Gamma_B$ has diameter at most $dL$.
  - Explanation: High minimum degree and expansion force logarithmic component diameter with probability tending to one.
- **thm-bounded-patterns** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-bounded-patterns)): There is a constant $\varepsilon>0$, depending only on the fixed alphabet and turn weights, with the following property. For every integer $K_0\geq1$, real number $C\geq1$, and integer $I\geq0$, the probability under $\mathbb P_L$ that $\Gamma$ contains a system satisfying all the following conditions tends to zero as $n\longrightarrow\infty$:* 1. *There are at most $K_0$ positive-length paths. Each is closed and cyclically immersed, except possibly one immersed interval path whose initial vertex is $x_A$ or $x_B$.* 2. *The total length satisfies $L\leq H\leq CL$.* 3. *There are at most $I$ comparisons, satisfying <a href="#eq:distinct-comparison-edges" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:distinct-comparison-edges">[eq:distinct-comparison-edges]</a>, and $b_0\leq\varepsilon H$.* *In particular, $\varepsilon$ is chosen before $K_0,C,I$. The threshold in $n$ and the rate of convergence may depend on these three bounds.
  - Explanation: Multiplicity stages and aligned word blocks exclude bounded path systems with a sufficiently small unpaired fraction; the fraction is fixed before the system bounds.
- **lem-bounded-image-rank** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-bounded-image-rank)): For fixed $C$, every finite graph of girth at least $L$ with at most $CL$ edges has bounded cycle rank, uniformly in $L$.
  - Explanation: Girth and a linear edge budget constrain cycle rank uniformly in the girth parameter.
- **lem-multiplicity-incidence** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-multiplicity-incidence)): The stage graphs satisfy $$\label{eq:stage-incidence} \sum_{j=1}^{m_*}E_j=H,\qquad \sum_{j=1}^{m_*}V_j\leq H+\iota,\qquad \sum_{j=1}^{m_*}(V_j-E_j-k_j)\leq0.$$
  - Explanation: Traversal incidence controls the total vertex count across multiplicity stages and removes a positive net vertex-choice exponent.
- **lem-link-word-counts** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-link-word-counts)): There is a number $\rho_L\to0$, uniform over patterns, with the following properties. Every word assignment counted in this lemma is required to be reduced on each block.* 1. *Given the reduced word on one block of a link component $\mathcal U$, at most $\exp(\rho_Ls\|\mathcal U\|)$ assignments of words to all its blocks satisfy the link constraints.* 2. *If $\mathcal U$ contains a self-link, the total number of such assignments is at most $\exp(\rho_Ls\|\mathcal U\|)$.* 3. *If $\mathcal U$ has no self-link, then $$\label{eq:component-multiplicity} 2z_{\mathcal U}\leq S_{\mathcal U}+b_{\mathcal U}.$$
  - Explanation: Link constraints determine almost all block letters; self-links remove the free initial word and ordinary components obey a multiplicity inequality.
- **thm-no-arrangements** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-no-arrangements)): In the random matching model of Section <a href="#sec:random" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:random">3</a>, conditioned on girth at least $L$, the probability that $\Gamma$ admits a reduced spherical arrangement tends to zero as $m\equiv1\pmod4$ tends to infinity. Consequently, for every sufficiently large admissible $m$ there is a matching outcome with girth at least $L$, the component diameter bound of Proposition <a href="#prop:diameter" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:diameter">6</a>, and no reduced spherical arrangement.
  - Explanation: Planar extraction converts every reduced spherical arrangement into a bounded path system, so the conditioned construction excludes all such arrangements asymptotically.
- **lem-closure** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-closure)): There is a fixed integer $D_0$, depending only on $d$ and $c_0$, with the following property for all sufficiently large admissible $n$. If every component of $\Gamma$ has diameter at most $dL$, then every positive-length immersed path segment in $\Gamma$ extends to a cyclically immersed closed path by appending at most $D_0L$ edges. The original segment is retained without cancellation. One may take any integer $$D_0\ge d+\frac{8}{c_0\log2}+8.$$
  - Explanation: Short based loops and a connecting geodesic close an immersed segment without canceling its original occurrences.
- **lem-paired-intervals** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-paired-intervals)): Suppose a reduced spherical arrangement with $N_0$ disk boundaries is divided into $N'\ge N_0$ pieces by cutting its paths into consecutive segments. An intact path counts as one piece, and no piece crosses the exceptional break. Then all original occurrence pairings can be represented by at most $6N'$ disjoint pairs of intervals. Each interval is contained in one designated piece, and each pair compares inverse strings in reverse boundary order. The intervals can also be required not to cross one prescribed indexing gap between occurrences on each original path, using the break as the exceptional indexing gap.
  - Explanation: An Euler characteristic count bounds exceptional gaps and organizes the planar pairing into inverse interval comparisons.
- **lem-separator** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-separator)): Let $$C_{\mathrm{sep}}=\frac{2\sqrt2}{1-\sqrt{2/3}}.$$ For every integer $K_0\ge1$, a finite planar multigraph with $N'$ vertices has a set of at most $C_{\mathrm{sep}}N'/\sqrt{K_0}$ vertices whose deletion leaves every connected component with at most $K_0$ vertices.
  - Explanation: Recursive planar separation controls the total vertex loss while bounding every remaining component.
- **prop-cone-criterion** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-cone-criterion)): Let $\Gamma=\Gamma_A\sqcup\Gamma_B$ be a finite graph immersed in the rose $F$, with chosen roots $x_A,x_B$, and let $X$ and $G$ be defined by <a href="#eq:cone-complex" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cone-complex">[eq:cone-complex]</a>. If $\Gamma$ admits no reduced spherical arrangement as in Definition <a href="#def:reduced-arrangement" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:reduced-arrangement">11</a>, then $$\pi_2(X)=0 \quad\text{and}\quad \text{the root-protection condition \eqref{eq:root-protection} holds}.$$ Moreover, $X$ is a finite aspherical complex of dimension at most two, and $G$ is finitely presented and torsion-free.
  - Explanation: Minimal cone pictures and band surgeries turn either a nontrivial sphere or a failed protected root into an excluded arrangement; asphericity then implies torsion-freeness.
- **lem-cone-pictures** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cone-pictures)): Every map $S^2\to X$ is homotopic to a cone picture. Every disk map whose boundary is the label of a specified edge path in $\Gamma$ is homotopic relative to that boundary to a cone picture.
  - Explanation: Relative replacement by presentation disks represents sphere and disk maps by cone pictures while preserving a prescribed boundary.

Suppose that $$\\label{eq:parity-assumptions}\n \\begin{aligned}\n |S_x\\cap S_{x'}|&\\equiv1\\pmod2\n   &&\\bigl(x,x'\\in A',\\ (x,x')\\ne(x_A,x_A)\\bigr),\\\\\n |S_{x_A}|&\\equiv0\\pmod2,\\\\\n |S_x\\cap S_y|&\\equiv1\\pmod2\n   &&\\bigl(x\\in A',\\ y\\in B'\\bigr).\n \\end{aligned}$$ Suppose also that the roots are protected in the following sense: $$\\label{eq:root-protection}\n \\begin{gathered}\n \\text{if a path in $\\Gamma$ starts at $x_A$ or $x_B$ and ends}\\\\[-2pt]\n \\text{at a different vertex, its label is nontrivial in $G$.}\n \\end{gathered}$$ Then the finite scalar sums $$\\label{eq:scalar-elements}\n a=\\sum_{x\\in A'}g_x,\\qquad\n b=\\sum_{x\\in A'}g_x^{-1},\\qquad\n c=\\sum_{y\\in B'}h_y^{-1}\n \\quad\\text{in }\\mathbb F_2[G]$$ satisfy $ab=1$, $ac=0$, and $c\\ne0$.       "conclusion": {
```json
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-types",
        "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large $m\\equiv1\\pmod4$, the prescribed sets $S_x\\subseteq T$, $x\\in A\\sqcup B$, satisfy the following identities. For every $Y\\in\\{A,B\\}$ and $t\\in T$, $$\\label{eq:label-balance}\n \\bigl|\\{x\\in Y:t\\in S_x\\}\\bigr|=|Y|p.$$ Their intersection cardinalities satisfy $$\\label{eq:type-parity}\n \\begin{aligned}\n |S_x\\cap S_{x'}|&\\equiv1\\pmod2\n &&\\bigl(x,x'\\in A,\\ (x,x')\\ne(x_A,x_A)\\bigr),\\\\\n |S_{x_A}|&\\equiv0\\pmod2,\\\\\n |S_x\\cap S_y|&\\equiv1\\pmod2\n &&\\bigl(x\\in A,\\ y\\in B\\bigr).\n \\end{aligned}$$ Moreover, $129\\le |S_x|\\le136$ at every vertex.",
        "source_id": "PM-197.1"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-197.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-prescriptions",
        "exact_quote": "There is a nonnegative sequence $r_n=vp+O(d_*^{2L+2})=o(n)$ with the following property. If $\\mathcal S$ is a collection of $s$ distinct prescriptions having positive probability under $\\mathbb P_L$, and $e$ is a further prescription not in $\\mathcal S$, then $$\\label{eq:one-prescription}\n \\mathbb P_L(e\\mid\\mathcal S)\n \\le\\frac1{np-s-r_n}\n \\qquad\\text{whenever }np-s-r_n>0.$$ In particular, for every fixed $C_1>0$, a specified collection of $E\\le C_1L$ distinct prescriptions satisfies the uniform bound $$\\label{eq:short-prescriptions}\n \\mathbb P_L(\\text{all }E\\text{ prescriptions hold})\n \\le (np)^{-E}\\exp(o(L)).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-197.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-prescriptions",
          "exact_quote": "There is a nonnegative sequence $r_n=vp+O(d_*^{2L+2})=o(n)$ with the following property.           "source_id": "PM-197.1"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-bounded-patterns",
        "exact_quote": "There is a constant $\\varepsilon>0$, depending only on the fixed alphabet and turn weights, with the following property. For every integer $K_0\\geq1$, real number $C\\geq1$, and integer $I\\geq0$, the probability under $\\mathbb P_L$ that $\\Gamma$ contains a system satisfying all the following conditions tends to zero as $n\\longrightarrow\\infty$:*\n\n1.  *There are at most $K_0$ positive-length paths. Each is closed and cyclically immersed, except possibly one immersed interval path whose initial vertex is $x_A$ or $x_B$.*\n\n2.  *The total length satisfies $L\\leq H\\leq CL$.*\n\n3.  *There are at most $I$ comparisons, satisfying <a href=\"#eq:distinct-comparison-edges\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:distinct-comparison-edges\">[eq:distinct-comparison-edges]</a>, and $b_0\\leq\\varepsilon H$.*\n\n*In particular, $\\varepsilon$ is chosen before $K_0,C,I$. The threshold in $n$ and the rate of convergence may depend on these three bounds.",
        "source_id": "PM-197.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-bounded-patterns",
          "exact_quote": "There is a constant $\\varepsilon>0$, depending only on the fixed alphabet and turn weights, with the following property. Each is closed and cyclically immersed, except possibly one immersed interval path whose initial vertex is $x_A$ or $x_B$.*\n\n2.  *The total length satisfies $L\\leq H\\leq CL$.*\n\n3.  *There are at most $I$ comparisons, satisfying <a href=\"#eq:distinct-comparison-edges\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:distinct-comparison-edges\">[eq:distinct-comparison-edges]</a>, and $b_0\\leq\\varepsilon H$.*\n\n*In particular, $\\varepsilon$ is chosen before $K_0,C,I$. The threshold in $n$ and the rate of convergence may depend on these three bounds.",
          "source_id": "PM-197.1"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-link-word-counts",
        "exact_quote": "There is a number $\\rho_L\\to0$, uniform over patterns, with the following properties. Every word assignment counted in this lemma is required to be reduced on each block.*\n\n1.  *Given the reduced word on one block of a link component $\\mathcal U$, at most $\\exp(\\rho_Ls|\\mathcal U|)$ assignments of words to all its blocks satisfy the link constraints.*\n\n2.  *If $\\mathcal U$ contains a self-link, the total number of such assignments is at most $\\exp(\\rho_Ls|\\mathcal U|)$.*\n\n\n3.  *If $\\mathcal U$ has no self-link, then $$\\label{eq:component-multiplicity}\n      2z_{\\mathcal U}\\leq S_{\\mathcal U}+b_{\\mathcal U}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-197.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-197.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-no-arrangements",
        "exact_quote": "In the random matching model of Section <a href=\"#sec:random\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:random\">3</a>, conditioned on girth at least $L$, the probability that $\\Gamma$ admits a reduced spherical arrangement tends to zero as $m\\equiv1\\pmod4$ tends to infinity. Consequently, for every sufficiently large admissible $m$ there is a matching outcome with girth at least $L$, the component diameter bound of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:diameter\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:diameter\">6</a>, and no reduced spherical arrangement.",
        "source_id": "PM-197.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-closure",
        "exact_quote": "There is a fixed integer $D_0$, depending only on $d$ and $c_0$, with the following property for all sufficiently large admissible $n$. If every component of $\\Gamma$ has diameter at most $dL$, then every positive-length immersed path segment in $\\Gamma$ extends to a cyclically immersed closed path by appending at most $D_0L$ edges. The original segment is retained without cancellation. One may take any integer $$D_0\\ge d+\\frac{8}{c_0\\log2}+8.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-197.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-paired-intervals",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose a reduced spherical arrangement with $N_0$ disk boundaries is divided into $N'\\ge N_0$ pieces by cutting its paths into consecutive segments. An intact path counts as one piece, and no piece crosses the exceptional break. Then all original occurrence pairings can be represented by at most $6N'$ disjoint pairs of intervals. Each interval is contained in one designated piece, and each pair compares inverse strings in reverse boundary order. The intervals can also be required not to cross one prescribed indexing gap between occurrences on each original path, using the break as the exceptional indexing gap.",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-cone-criterion",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Gamma=\\Gamma_A\\sqcup\\Gamma_B$ be a finite graph immersed in the rose $F$, with chosen roots $x_A,x_B$, and let $X$ and $G$ be defined by <a href=\"#eq:cone-complex\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cone-complex\">[eq:cone-complex]</a>. If $\\Gamma$ admits no reduced spherical arrangement as in Definition <a href=\"#def:reduced-arrangement\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:reduced-arrangement\">11</a>, then $$\\pi_2(X)=0\n \\quad\\text{and}\\quad\n \\text{the root-protection condition \\eqref{eq:root-protection} holds}.$$ Moreover, $X$ is a finite aspherical complex of dimension at most two, and $G$ is finitely presented and torsion-free.",
        "source_id": "PM-197.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-cone-criterion",

          "source_id": "PM-197.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\Gamma=\\Gamma_A\\sqcup\\Gamma_B$ be a finite graph immersed in the rose $F$, with chosen roots $x_A,x_B$, and let $X$ and $G$ be defined by <a href=\"#eq:cone-complex\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cone-complex\">[eq:cone-complex]</a>.           "exact_quote": "Every map $S^2\\to X$ is homotopic to a cone picture. Every disk map whose boundary is the label of a specified edge path in $\\Gamma$ is homotopic relative to that boundary to a cone picture.",
          "source_id": "PM-197.1"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-cone-pictures"
    }
  ],
  "generation_status": "COMPLETE",
  "pm_id": "PM-197.1",
  "schema": "pm.claims.v2"
}
```

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Directly finite ring** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A unital ring $R$ is *directly finite* if $ab=1$ implies $ba=1$ for all $a,b\in R$.
- **Group algebra** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a field $K$ and a group $G$, the group algebra $K[G]$ consists of finite formal sums of elements of $G$ with coefficients in $K$, with multiplication extending the group operation bilinearly.
- **Classifying complex** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A classifying complex for $G$ is a connected CW complex with fundamental group $G$ and contractible universal cover.
- **Signed alphabet** ([source](#sec:algebra)): A *signed alphabet* is a finite set $T$ with a fixed-point-free involution $t\mapsto\bar t$.
- **Labeled graph** ([source](#sec:algebra)): A $T$-labeled graph $\Gamma$ has a label on every oriented edge, with opposite orientations carrying inverse labels.
- **Immersion** ([source](#sec:algebra)): The map is an *immersion* if the labels on the edges leaving any one vertex are distinct.
- **Path length** ([source](#sec:algebra)): The length of an edge path is its number of edge traversals, counted with multiplicity.
- **Immersed path** ([source](#sec:algebra)): A path is *immersed* if it has no consecutive edge and reverse edge.
- **Cyclic immersion** ([source](#sec:algebra)): A closed path is *cyclically immersed* if this also holds at the closing turn.
- **Match prescription** ([source](#pm-h-9)): A *match prescription* specifies one matched pair in one of the bijections, on a specified side $A$ or $B$.
- **Comparison** ([source](#sec:patterns)): A comparison consists of two disjoint intervals of positions of equal length, together with one of the following requirements: their words agree in the given orders, or their words are $t_1\cdots t_h$ and $\bar t_h\cdots\bar t_1$.
- **Cycle rank** ([source](#pm-h-12)): Here the cycle rank of a finite graph is $\|E\|-\|V\|+$ the number of connected components.
- **Stage graph** ([source](#pm-h-12)): Its *stage $j$ graph* $\Delta_j$ consists of all edges with $m_e\geq j$ and their endpoints, for $1\leq j\leq m_*$.
- **Cone complex and cone group** ([source](#eq:cone-complex)): For each connected component $\Lambda$ of $\Gamma$, let $K_\Lambda$ be its abstract cone, and form $$\label{eq:cone-complex} X=F\mathop{\cup}_{\Lambda\to F} \bigcup_{\Lambda\in\pi_0(\Gamma)}K_\Lambda, \qquad G=\pi_1(X,*).$$
- **Reduced spherical arrangement** ([source](#stmt-def-reduced-arrangement)): A *reduced spherical arrangement over $\Gamma$* consists of finitely many pairwise disjoint piecewise linear closed disks in an oriented sphere, together with the following data. Each boundary reads a positive-length cyclically immersed closed path in $\Gamma$, except possibly one boundary. That boundary reads a positive-length immersed interval path starting at $x_A$ or $x_B$, with its two endpoints represented at a single marked boundary point, called the *break*. No immersion condition is imposed across the break. At least one ordinary, cyclically immersed boundary is required. The paths are read in the boundary orientations induced by the disks. Their edge occurrences are paired in distinct twos by mutually disjoint piecewise linear arcs. The interiors of the arcs lie outside the disks, and each occurrence contains exactly one arc endpoint in its interior. Paired occurrences have inverse signed letters and use distinct underlying undirected edges of $\Gamma$.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **thm-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cone-pictures-2): A right inverse exists for the constructed group-algebra element, while the nonzero annihilated element excludes a left inverse. -> A right inverse exists for the constructed group-algebra element, while the nonzero annihilated element excludes a left inverse.
- **prop-parity** [claim](#stmt-prop-parity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-parity): Odd-degree component parity cancels the mixed product and leaves the identity in the self product; root protection preserves a nonzero coefficient. -> Odd-degree component parity cancels the mixed product and leaves the identity in the self product; root protection preserves a nonzero coefficient.
- **prop-types** [claim](#stmt-prop-types) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-types): Projective-plane incidences and complements of Fano lines give balanced label counts and the distinguished parity exception. -> Projective-plane incidences and complements of Fano lines give balanced label counts and the distinguished parity exception.
- **lem-word-decay** [claim](#stmt-lem-word-decay) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-word-decay): A positive vector bounds the squared turn-weight matrix by a strict contraction, giving exponential decay. -> A positive vector bounds the squared turn-weight matrix by a strict contraction, giving exponential decay.
- **lem-prescriptions** [claim](#stmt-lem-prescriptions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-prescriptions): Injective switching bounds additional edge prescriptions under girth conditioning, without a lower bound on the conditioning probability. -> Injective switching bounds additional edge prescriptions under girth conditioning, without a lower bound on the conditioning probability.
- **prop-diameter** [claim](#stmt-prop-diameter) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-diameter): High minimum degree and expansion force logarithmic component diameter with probability tending to one. -> High minimum degree and expansion force logarithmic component diameter with probability tending to one.
- **thm-bounded-patterns** [claim](#stmt-thm-bounded-patterns) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-link-word-counts-2): Multiplicity stages and aligned word blocks exclude bounded path systems with a sufficiently small unpaired fraction; the fraction is fixed before the system bounds. -> Multiplicity stages and aligned word blocks exclude bounded path systems with a sufficiently small unpaired fraction; the fraction is fixed before the system bounds.
- **lem-bounded-image-rank** [claim](#stmt-lem-bounded-image-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bounded-image-rank): Girth and a linear edge budget constrain cycle rank uniformly in the girth parameter. -> Girth and a linear edge budget constrain cycle rank uniformly in the girth parameter.
- **lem-multiplicity-incidence** [claim](#stmt-lem-multiplicity-incidence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-multiplicity-incidence): Traversal incidence controls the total vertex count across multiplicity stages and removes a positive net vertex-choice exponent. -> Traversal incidence controls the total vertex count across multiplicity stages and removes a positive net vertex-choice exponent.
- **lem-link-word-counts** [claim](#stmt-lem-link-word-counts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-link-word-counts): Link constraints determine almost all block letters; self-links remove the free initial word and ordinary components obey a multiplicity inequality. -> Link constraints determine almost all block letters; self-links remove the free initial word and ordinary components obey a multiplicity inequality.
- **thm-no-arrangements** [claim](#stmt-thm-no-arrangements) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-separator-2): Planar extraction converts every reduced spherical arrangement into a bounded path system, so the conditioned construction excludes all such arrangements asymptotically. -> Planar extraction converts every reduced spherical arrangement into a bounded path system, so the conditioned construction excludes all such arrangements asymptotically.
- **lem-closure** [claim](#stmt-lem-closure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-closure): Short based loops and a connecting geodesic close an immersed segment without canceling its original occurrences. -> Short based loops and a connecting geodesic close an immersed segment without canceling its original occurrences.
- **lem-paired-intervals** [claim](#stmt-lem-paired-intervals) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-paired-intervals): An Euler characteristic count bounds exceptional gaps and organizes the planar pairing into inverse interval comparisons. -> An Euler characteristic count bounds exceptional gaps and organizes the planar pairing into inverse interval comparisons.
- **lem-separator** [claim](#stmt-lem-separator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-separator): Recursive planar separation controls the total vertex loss while bounding every remaining component. -> Recursive planar separation controls the total vertex loss while bounding every remaining component.
- **prop-cone-criterion** [claim](#stmt-prop-cone-criterion) / [proof](#pm-h-23): Minimal cone pictures and band surgeries turn either a nontrivial sphere or a failed protected root into an excluded arrangement; asphericity then implies torsion-freeness. -> Minimal cone pictures and band surgeries turn either a nontrivial sphere or a failed protected root into an excluded arrangement; asphericity then implies torsion-freeness.
- **lem-cone-pictures** [claim](#stmt-lem-cone-pictures) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cone-pictures): Relative replacement by presentation disks represents sphere and disk maps by cone pictures while preserving a prescribed boundary. -> Relative replacement by presentation disks represents sphere and disk maps by cone pictures while preserving a prescribed boundary.

<!-- PM:END pm.roadmap.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-FANO**: Fano-line complements have four elements and pairwise intersection size two; each extra letter occurs in four complements, and each pair occurs in two.
- **I-GROUP**: The constructed group algebra contains witnesses with $ab=1$, $ac=0$, and $c\ne0$, so $ba\ne1$.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **AU-WITNESS**: Treat finite incidence checks as a computed group-algebra counterexample. -> Balanced extra-label incidences do not establish asphericity, root protection, or the witness identities for a selected group.
- **AU-FIELD**: Transfer the witness identities to arbitrary coefficient fields. -> Parity cancellation relies on characteristic two.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The Fano-line complement family is finite and explicitly defined. This computation checks that family; the paper selects a matching outcome without listing its edges, so the full group witness remains outside the computation.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON batch of permutations of the seven nonzero binary vectors.
# PRECONDITIONS: each permutation contains exactly the integers from 1 through 7.
# OUTPUTS: sorted Fano-line complement blocks for each permutation.
# TIME: O(B * binomial(7,3) * 7) for batch size B.
# SPACE: O(B * 7 * 4) for the output.
# ARITHMETIC: exact integer XOR and python-flint fmpz cardinalities.
# FAILURE: malformed permutations raise ValueError and exit nonzero.
import json, sys
from itertools import combinations
from flint import fmpz
batch = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())
answer = []
for perm in batch:
    if sorted(perm) != list(range(1, 8)):
        raise ValueError("not a permutation")
    blocks = []
    for a, b, c in combinations(range(1, 8), 3):
        if a ^ b ^ c == 0:
            block = sorted(perm[x - 1] for x in range(1, 8) if x not in (a, b, c))
            if fmpz(len(block)) != 4:
                raise ValueError("wrong complement size")
            blocks.append(block)
    answer.append(sorted(blocks))
print(json.dumps(answer))
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Conditional transfer to symbolic group-algebra software: a faithful representation of the constructed group and scalar sums could expose an invalid inference from a one-sided inverse to a two-sided inverse. The selected edge list is not supplied. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the random matching model proves mathematical existence; no game rules, player model, or validated simulation application is established. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Conditional transfer to algebraic verification tools: the nonzero annihilated element obstructs reverse invertibility once the witness and its ambient group are faithfully encoded. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Conditional transfer to finite graph validation: the simultaneous-step construction reduces the algebraic parity argument to connected components and degree parity. Root protection remains a separate geometric premise. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Conditional transfer to labeled multigraph representations: the matching model distinguishes label slots even when their vertices coincide, and the parity argument retains parallel simultaneous steps. Collapsing these incidences changes the construction. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: no accelerator architecture, hardware implementation, or performance result is established for the finite matching construction. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the group-algebra counterexample establishes an algebraic failure of reverse invertibility without a cryptographic protocol or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the conditioned random matching estimates are mathematical probability bounds; no learning objective, training procedure, or predictive guarantee is established. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: planar separation extracts an excluded path system, without an optimisation objective or an operations-research contract. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: simultaneous steps couple coordinates of finite graph products; no distributed protocol, fault model, or replication guarantee is established. |
| other (group algebras and geometric group theory) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | Direct application to mathematical research on group algebras: the constructed torsion-free finitely presented group has a group algebra that fails direct finiteness and a finite two-dimensional classifying complex. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (counterexample_scope): The counterexample concerns the constructed finitely presented torsion-free group and the group algebra over the field of two elements. Blocked use: ['Do not extend these witness identities to arbitrary fields or coefficient rings.', 'Do not conclude that every finitely presented torsion-free group has a group algebra that fails direct finiteness.']
- **L2** (nonconstructive): The argument selects a finite matching outcome without listing its edges; finite scalar witnesses are obtained only after such an outcome is selected. Blocked use: ['Do not present the existence proof as an enumerated group presentation or a supplied executable witness.', 'Do not claim a runtime bound for finding an admissible matching outcome.']
- **L3** (hypothesis): The parity criterion requires finite loopless immersed graphs, the prescribed intersection parities, and root protection. Blocked use: ['Do not apply the parity conclusion to arbitrary labeled graphs.', 'Do not replace nonvanishing of the scalar sum by a check of the zero product alone.']
- **L4** (edge_case): Initial random matching outcomes may contain loops; the selected outcome uses the girth requirement to exclude loops and parallel edges. Blocked use: ['Do not apply the loopless parity criterion to an unrestricted matching sample.']
- **L5** (approximation): The diameter and arrangement-exclusion conclusions are asymptotic probability statements, rather than guarantees for every matching outcome. Blocked use: ['Do not certify a particular outcome by appealing only to convergence of its success probability.', 'Do not assert a supplied explicit threshold for the admissible size parameter.']
- **L6** (hypothesis): The conditioned matching law is used only after its conditioning event is shown to have positive probability. Blocked use: ['Do not treat an empty girth-conditioned sample space as a valid probability model.']
- **L7** (hypothesis): The permitted unpaired proportion is fixed before the bounds on path number, path length, and comparisons; the threshold and convergence rate may depend on those bounds. Blocked use: ['Do not interchange the parameter choices to assert uniform exclusion for growing path-system bounds.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We construct a finitely presented torsion-free counterexample to Kaplansky's direct finiteness conjecture over the field of two elements. The group admits a finite two-dimensional classifying complex.
